## 2026-11-20 02:27 UTC — known-hidden-printing re-determinization bug confirmed

- Read-only source check confirmed Agent19's post-trace concern. `sampleHiddenPartition()` in `simulation/twinleaf/2026-11/agent5-hidden-partition-sampler.cjs` conditions on printing multisets only; it has no physical-card-ID → known-printing constraint. `applySampledPartitionsToTwinleafState()` in `simulation/twinleaf/2026-11/agent5-twinleaf-belief-input.cjs` retains hidden card IDs but replaces each face from the sampled partition via `createCard(printing)`. ## 2026-11-16 07:00 UTC — clarify the frozen V3 worker-policy boundary

The V3 fixture config removes an ambiguity in the earlier source-level baseline note:

- `simulation/evaluation/2026-11/agent36-v3-runner.cjs` builds candidate and opponent identities from `agent46-candidate-policy.cjs` and `agent6-rank1-shadow-rider-opponent-policy.cjs`, with `candidatePolicyOptions:{}` and `opponentPolicyOptions:{}`. The run receipt labels them persistent actor-only Docker workers with **no in-process strategic fallback**; `candidateHoodOptionsApplied:false` and `candidateRecoveryAutonomyApplied:false`.
- `simulation/twinleaf/agent34-actor-only-root-policy.cjs` is the host-side actor-only role adapter and contains opt-in Underworld Door logic, but the fixture options do not enable `underworldDoorRoot`; its presence in the common role entrypoint is not evidence that the candidate gets that strategic fallback. Therefore the worker-policy asymmetry is genuinely relevant to the baseline: Agent46's `chooseRoot()` selects only `Resetting Hole` among root `use-ability` actions and does not select Underworld Door/Climactic Gate; Agent6's rank-1 worker explicitly scores Underworld Door and Climactic Gate.
- Agent46's `scoreBasicPlay()` also tests `!boardHas(view,'Shadow Rider Calyrex V')` while `hasName` uses substring matching; a Shadow Rider VMAX contains that prefix. This appears to prevent the worker from benching a second Shadow Rider V after any V/VMAX is already present. Treat this as a source-level hypothesis to confirm against the exact frozen worker version and first-game trace, not as a demonstrated gameplay outcome.
- These are pre-registered baseline asymmetries for trace interpretation only. Do not tune or replace either worker before the first accepted natural-terminal game. Trace the actual worker requests/responses, host role adapter, selected frontier actions and any rejection/fallback separately; don't attribute a final action to the wrong layer.

This clarification is based on the exact V3 fixture config at `df38788ed9de1781c59e5d18e955f3a1dfa6d616`, not the separate Agent46 policy file viewed in isolation.

Fairy Zone does not apply to Latias because it is Psychic, and Psychic is not its Weakness. At N≤6 or with ≥10 existing damage, Power Beam may be better; the decision depends on exact target, damage, retreat, Prize map and VMAX liability. ## 2026-11-16 17:15 UTC — trusted-host allowlist overbreadth veto on 264f984

- Read Agent36's 17:09 review and 17:10 exact-head follow-up plus Agent43's independent 17:10 review of PR #58 head `264f9847854aad5c5f411b284fb32ce7fc4f8232`. They found that the new exact-path exceptions remain overbroad because the scanner groups capabilities:
  - `usesProcessOrWorker` groups `child_process`, `worker_threads`, and `vm`; trusting the Docker launcher path currently allows all three, though it needs only `node:child_process`.
  - `usesFileReader` groups ordinary filesystem reads with `process.dlopen`, `process.binding`, and `require.extensions`; trusting the Docker and Aichi clock paths therefore allows loader/runtime operations in those modules too.
- Required narrow correction: split ordinary filesystem reads from loader/runtime APIs; trust ordinary reads only in the two exact host paths; allow only `node:child_process` in the exact Docker launcher; keep `worker_threads`, `vm`, `process.dlopen`, `process.binding`, and `require.extensions` rejected everywhere. Add negative tests for each forbidden capability inside otherwise trusted paths, not only in arbitrary untrusted modules.
- Agent35's 17:11 review of the positive host paths and arbitrary-module negatives said “no new finding” but did not cover the trusted-path negative matrix. Sent Agent49 a direct veto/summary at `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261116T171512Z_agent6-trusted-host-allowlist-overbreadth-veto.md` and Agent35 a review follow-up at `communications/agent35/2026-11/20261116T171512Z_agent6-review-followup-trusted-path-negative-cases.md`. ### Trace annotations: human-concept definitions

For the first accepted game, use the uploaded `human_concepts.md` as a heuristic vocabulary, not as an authoritative scoring model: **UDP** marks cards protected from discard by their intended role in the current line; **DCI** is the state- and matchup-dependent desirability of discarding a card (the source suggests 0 = preserve, 1 = discard promptly, but do not force a scalar where context is richer); **AMR** discounts technically available actions whose costs, Supporter contention, Bench occupancy or opponent timing make them unrealistic, while allowing an AMR override when delaying means losing access; **ALS** records exact multi-card sequences and counter-lines that an unordered decklist or generic archetype label misses. Keep **K0/K1** as analyst knowledge states after the first physical deck search, not as worker-observed information unless the actor's actual observation/history supports it. Source: `human_concepts.md` lines 19–98, 215–257 and 290–316.

## 2026-11-17 incarnation — offline first-trace replay joiner

Reclaimed agent6 at 2026-11-17T05:48:45.945124Z after the prior incarnation's lease had been released. The first-trace research program remains unchanged: no deck/policy edits and no fixture dispatch before the canonical V3 acceptance gates pass.

- tests/2026-11/agent6-v3-trace-replay-join.test.cjs

- handoff to Agent14 at communications/agent14/2026-11/20261117T061956Z_agent6-offline-replay-joiner.md

The joiner is a post-acceptance offline diagnostic, not a runner. It compares the original sanitized artifact with a separately sanitized offline-decrypted replay (generated using the exact same sanitizer revision) and requires identical trial/seed/seat bindings, printed-60 hashes, engine/research pins, policy identities, sanitized public artifact (apart from ephemeral worker container identifiers and derived audit digest), game trace/summary, clean worker audits, zero policy RNG draws/violations, valid actor-only boundary, and natural terminal. It joins generic SelectPrompt rows by action ordinal/seat/turn/type, with prompt ID secondary, searches the private snapshot for WANT_TO_DRAW_CARDS, and emits only safe option counts/labels, numeric choice evidence, and hand counts. A draw count is reported only when the unique direct numeric draw-value evidence agrees with the accepted hand-count delta; index-only or conflicting values remain unresolved. No raw prompt/card/token/seed/full-state data is emitted.

Validation: the updated helper and its 14 synthetic contract tests passed locally. The first version imported the sanitizer module, but that dependency was removed after confirming the module exists on PR #58 and not main. This is not an integration test against a real accepted artifact; the first game has not been run, and the helper has not been exercised against real replay data. It does not create competitive evidence. The source-level prompt analysis remains a deterministic-reconstruction path only.

## 2026-11-18T17:13:17.850Z — likely root cause: seed-probe bypasses ChooseCardsPrompt decode/validate

- Found a concrete likely cause for the 64/64 `opponentActiveStateMismatch` on run 44351760735. The current seed-probe `ChooseCardsPrompt` handler computes `selected` as card indices and directly dispatches `new ResolvePromptAction(prompt.id, selected)`.
- At pinned Twinleaf SHA `a9905a76a6c4103ba4b6cb9a49e86bad795c5c05`, `ChooseCardsPrompt extends Prompt<Card[]>`; its `decode(number[])` maps indices to actual `Card[]`; its `validate(Card[])` checks constraints. Pinned `GameSocket.resolvePrompt` (blob `b89ecdba0b8f272dea12e7c352cc2d43e572a67f`, around lines 265–285) performs `prompt.decode(params.result, game.state)`, then `prompt.validate(...)`, before constructing `ResolvePromptAction`. Pinned `Store` direct action path does not decode; it passes `action.result` straight to the prompt callback. `setupSinglePlayer()` callback expects `Card[]` and passes it to `putStartingPokemonsAndPrizes()`, which moves `cards[0]` into Active. Raw indices therefore can fail to populate Active while appearing to resolve the prompt; a subsequent opponent `min:0` extra-bench prompt then triggers the active-state guard. This explains the observed failure far better than the earlier occupancy hypotheses, but remains unverified until a rerun.
- Recommended exact repair to Agent49 in `communications/agent49/2026-11/20261118T1715Z_agent6-seed-probe-decode-validation-root-cause.md`, commit `1357a24c5772032b0ac68fb49427bc7d78683270`: `decoded=prompt.decode(selected, request.state)`; require `prompt.validate(decoded, request.state) !== false`; dispatch `ResolvePromptAction(prompt.id, decoded)`. Catch exceptions and preserve only safe prompt identity/reason. Add regression that decoded result is passed, with no card names/IDs in logs. `SelectPrompt` and `ConfirmPrompt` do not override base decode/validate, so their index/boolean results remain unchanged.
- Updated decision board `results/README.md` in commit `59623e0229c4966f82cc1dacba93911c9a54ffc4` with the likely root cause, exact pinned-source evidence, and gate sequence. Agent49 remains sole implementation/dispatch owner; do not change branch or dispatch. Current head `fbaa2b84d89933b0e86a995111152a5bab93d265` still has no Actions gate; next step is decode/validate fix, exact-head contract-only, then native no-game. Keep Active-state invariant and all registered inputs unchanged.


## 2026-11-18T17:15:57.297Z — normal V3 prompt dispatcher has the same raw-result bug

## 2026-11-18T17:17:31.181Z — CardList behavior confirms raw indices cannot populate Active

- Verified pinned `ptcg-server/src/game/store/state/card-list.ts`: `moveCardTo(card: Card, destination)` delegates to `moveCardsTo([card], destination)`; `moveCardsTo` searches by object identity via `this.cards.indexOf(cards[i])` and silently skips a missing object. Thus raw numeric indices passed to `setupSinglePlayer()` are not converted to Cards and cannot move the chosen starting Pokémon to Active.
